# Metrum Rise — Roadmap

Active tracked work uses stable IDs instead of positional numbering. This file is the live planning surface for priorities, validated bugs, and later tracks.

Status values:
- `open`
- `in_progress`
- `needs_revalidation`
- `done`
- `parked`
- `superseded` (replaced by the tracked item named in its entry)

Priority values:
- `P0`
- `P1`
- `P2`

Kind values:
- `feature`
- `bug`
- `hardening`
- `refactor`
- `docs`

## Active Priorities

Simulation engine: **`SIM-02` is done (2026-10-22)**. Its scheduler remains authoritative for
the road refactor. `PERF-07` accepts 30k at both speeds and 100k at 1× on unpreempted frames;
100k at 32× remains `PERF-08`. See the [engine contract](simulation.md#sim-02-scheduler-refactor).

**`ROAD-68` — `refactor`, `in_progress`, `P1` (2026-10-23; every gate `A`-`H` closed on branch
`road68-H` 2026-10-29, `ROAD-68-H` round 6; done when `road` takes it):** replace the legacy road-surface
compiler (`simulation/network/surface/`, about 145k lines) with a constructive roadbed. Junctions
are built directly from their arms: sorted arms, curb-return corners, round joins and mouth
stations, with heights `plane + level` and no booleans, noding or height authority. Spans and nodes
share mouth sections. The existing terrain CDT is fed oriented outer chains. Preview is
synchronous, commit adopts the preview's pieces, and undo swaps them back. Targets:
- the exact road-and-terrain preview renders within 16.7 ms of the sampled input
- the commit is acknowledged within one frame at 100k buildings
- `roadbed/` stays within 8,000 production lines

The game may break between gates on branch `road`, and save compatibility is not kept. Gate
`ROAD-68-A` (remove legacy, add the façade) is done: the legacy compiler is deleted and consumers
use the roadbed façade. `ROAD-68-B` (profiles and spans) is done: span pieces render between the
graph clips, queries answer from their triangles, and buildings place again; a 100 m span builds in
0.017 ms. `ROAD-68-C` (junction layout and node pieces) is done, with rendered reference screenshots: bends,
junctions and terminals render as node pieces built from their layouts, graph clips and the
profile hard zone come from the layout mouths, and layout failures reject placements with
reasons; a four-way node piece builds in 0.071 ms and a six-way in 0.127 ms. Its follow-up
(2026-10-24, user decisions D1 and D8) tightens curb returns to 3.5 m and moves the pedestrian
crossings onto the layout: zebras sit right outside the curves, sidewalk corner walks follow the
layout's sidewalk. A second follow-up (2026-10-24, user decision "keep the old behaviour",
open question 12) restores one crossing at every degree-two node: on a bend's node piece right
outside the curve, and on a pass-through node's span beside the node, so walkers can change sides
at every node again and the walking planner's whole-network proof holds. Pre-D work (2026-10-24,
open questions 15 and 9): physical profiles, clips and pieces derive from the final graph only,
so flat reference junctions no longer depend on drawing order (slopes remain open question 16),
and straight width changes taper along both approaches (D4). Footpaths meeting roads join the
sidewalk ring at sidewalk height on shared vertices (D5, open question 14), footpath-only nodes
lay out as ordinary arms, footpath lanes end at their layout mouths and join the corner walks,
and a bend whose crossing does not fit on its first arm crosses on the other (open question 13).
The pre-D review fixes (2026-10-24) keep a crossable arm's zebra at the corner when a footpath
beside it moves its mouth (open question 17, user decision), order footpaths at road ends along
their sector, rank profile corridors by a documented geometric rule, re-solve profiles after node
moves and road removals, and turn the remaining silent skips into placement rejections.
Before `ROAD-68-E` the roadbed was compacted for quality (2026-10-24, user decision): duplication,
dead code and needless parameters removed, `roadbed/` at 7,229 production lines (7,545 before),
with every product bitwise unchanged under a product-hash probe that later gates keep.
`ROAD-68-G` (2026-10-24, D7; done 2026-10-29 with part 10, below) is implemented: a commit, undo, road bulldoze or other topology edit
rebuilds the CCH as a pure job on a graph copy and adopts it one frame later, the simulation frame
holding meanwhile, deterministically across worker counts, a late rebuild and a save in the hold.
That takes the CCH build (312 ms at 100k residents) off the commit. Part 2 found the measured
2.4 s was the breakdown harness never acknowledging an upload; with acknowledgements, the render
publication now re-emits only the rebuilt pieces, the entrance rebuild derives only the changed
entrances and the agent pass runs in parallel, bit for bit as before: 41 ms at 100k (78 ms
before). Part 3 made the commit's cell lots follow the roads whose geometry or curb frames
changed and made cell generation cheaper, products bit for bit unchanged: 28.6 ms at 100k
(41.7 ms in the same session's control). Part 4 fixed a difference in part 3's marks (undo,
which leaves its regions to the `cell lots` queue, queued fewer of them; every edit but the
commit marks the whole ring again, checked by a digest test over every edit kind), then lifted
each new lane once, spliced the publication into reserved buffers, invalidated cells without
the lots' frontage reach and made the agent passes' set probes cheap, products and determinism
digests bit for bit unchanged: median 21.8 ms at 100k (18.3–28.3 per sample; same-session
control 41.8). Part 5 (2026-10-25) fixed a deadlock in pure jobs (a frame waited for the
routing rebuild on a pool worker; the frame's caller now finishes it before entering the pool),
put the new-lane lift and chunk seal back on the pool and made the cell road-overlap test exact
without rounding: median 19.3 ms at 100k before the `ROAD-68-D` rebase (control 41.6). On
`road` after `ROAD-68-D` the 100k commit was rejected and the commit's terrain validation cost
15–69 ms, in the control too; `ROAD-68-D` part 6 fixes both (below). `ROAD-68-G` part 6
(2026-10-25) showed the
slowest, first sample is the larger edit beside the painted grid, not a cold start, and put its
reattachment search, street lights and signs and retained frontage lines on the pool, bit for
bit: 20.5 / 17.9 / 16.0 ms per sample at 100k before the rebase (control 23.4 / 19.2 / 13.7).
It still misses
a frame (open question 21: snapshot publish and copy, which `ROAD-68-E`'s road-tool context
owns, then the lots, publication and agent passes), so its acceptance stays open. Part 7
(2026-10-27, branch `road68-G7` on `terrain07-E`, WIP) builds question 33 (c): the commit's lots
are planned in a pure job and adopted by the next frame, bitwise as planning in the commit, and
the commit's background jobs start last, its changed chunks splice on the pool and its graph
copy is cloned on the pool. In its landing session the first commits fall from 25.8-29.7 ms to
16.0-17.3 ms at 10k-100k and every other sample takes 3.0-11.8 ms; the first 100k sample (17.3,
16.9 ms) still misses, with the legacy refined patches' road ownership (3.6-4.2 ms, removed by
`TERRAIN-07-D`/`-G`) its largest stage. Part 8 (WIP) starts the routing rebuild after the lot
plan and moves the adoption tick a frame after a commit that deferred its lots (roads.md question
58, taken pending the user's review): adoption frames take 0.3-2.3 ms; first commits 14.9-17.9
ms, three of twelve populated first samples still over 16.7 ms. Its populated-map locality
check keeps the commit's products bitwise from 0 to 100k remote sites, but at 100k the commit
costs about 3 ms more and the adoption's attachment repair 3-6 ms more, so locality stays open. Part 9
(2026-10-28, branch `road68-G9`, WIP) rebases parts 7 and 8 onto gate E and makes the adoption's
attachment repair test only the buildings in the 512 m chunks its edit reaches, checked against
the whole city's test in test builds: flat from 0 to 100k remote sites (0.07-0.17 ms, 2.8-8.1
before). First commits 14.4-18.2 ms (control 24.5-32.3), three of twelve populated first samples
still over 16.7 ms; the agent passes and the graph copy still grow with the city (roads.md
questions 59 and 60). Part 10 (2026-10-29, branch `road68-G10` on `TERRAIN-07-G`) closes the
gate: the rebase drops the legacy terrain state with `TERRAIN-07-G`, and the commit queues its
undo record's kept terrain tiles after its last stage, so their bake no longer takes the commit's
cores. Every populated first commit takes 9.6-13.8 ms at 10k-100k (control 21.1-27.5) and every
later sample at most 7.5 ms; products, locality products and agent records (1, 3, 4 and 8
workers, a late rebuild, a save in the hold) bitwise. The user's decisions of 2026-10-28 are
recorded (questions 58 (b), 59 (b) in its own round with `PERF-07` after this gate, 60 (a)) and
carried to `ROAD-68-H` with the commit's locality growth at 100k remote sites (1.6-3.1 ms) and the
economy contract's fiscal hour means (1-4 % above the control, work identical, no limit crossed).
`ROAD-68-H` round 1 (2026-10-29, branch `road68-H`, in progress): the dead paths gates D-G and
`TERRAIN-07-G` left are removed with every product bitwise; `./run.sh --test` runs every rendered
test in gamescope's headless backend and skips nothing (the vegetation level match test, unrun
since the weather landed, now clears the default cloud deck); the commit row passes (first
samples 6.2-14.6 ms at 0-100k), and the fiscal rise is layout by profile. Round 2 (2026-10-29):
the user accepted the commit's locality as measured (later tracks `ROAD-71`, `TERRAIN-11`, and
`ROAD-72` for the topology stage's +0.44 ms, a split scanning every building); the rendered preview
rows' late poses run the same work at the P-cores' idle clock (1.2 GHz against 1.7-2.4 on time;
70 % on time with the Method's settings, 85-90 % without the FPS cap), not presentation pacing
(`roads.md` question 62); the populated row with the simulation active (question 61 (a), built)
misses at 100k, 0 of 590 within 16.7 ms: each pose re-emits whole 512 m road chunks (11.5 MB) and
the engine's frame time is partly unattributed (question 63). The user decided (2026-10-29) the
next round's work: the Method drops the FPS cap and reports Wayland beside Xwayland, the pose's
main-thread work is cut to fit the idle clock (62 (d), (b)), and preview payloads go below the
chunk (63 (b)), targets unchanged; the
routing adoption frame's 125 ms agent tick is the movement scratch re-prepared for the new
revision and the waiting replans (`PERF-11`); the drag probe passes 39 of 39 strokes with
preview page waits apart from pool exhaustion, its long frames the CPU-bound render frame
(`RENDER-27`); carried items 5 (culled gap +0.279 ms against +0.42) and 6 (clearance) pass, and 9 puts the terrain query's snapshot share at +0.23 ms beside the control's own `PERF-09` guard miss. Round 3 (2026-10-29): sub-chunk preview payloads (the rebuilt pieces and the resident runs they replace; 11.5 -> 2.8 MB a pose at 100k, every product and 43 captures bitwise), the view holds every published chunk, `ROAD-72` done; the paused rows 78.7 % / 90.4 % (Xwayland / Wayland, no cap) and the populated row 27-29 % active still miss (question 62 (b) carried; question 64 open: the graded renderer's streaming after the load); carried item 1 accepted by the user; the terrain tangent warning fixed; the gate blocked by `TERRAIN-12` (holes where the previous preview pose lay, found by the user in play). Rounds 4-5 (2026-10-29): `TERRAIN-12`'s holes fixed in the Rust staging (T60, T61 (1)), the undo's frame by holding an install's replaced chunks until its graded edit draws (T61 (2)); T62 (a)'s held junction runs double and z-fight, so the gate waits for the user's T64 and a 9.67 ms populated drag pose frame; the commit row (at most 12.01 ms at 100k) and the bitwise rows pass; the rendered preview rows are carried to `TERRAIN-13` and `RENDER-30` (question 65). Round 6 (2026-10-29): by the user's T64 decision (about 21:45) the junction hold is removed (round 4's united junction, 43 captures identical) and the undo hold kept; `TERRAIN-12` done for the hole classes fixed, its junction and fast-click frames carried to the root-cause question T65; the drag frames under background adoption jobs carried to `PERF-12`; the commit row (at most 13.79 ms at 100k) and the bitwise rows pass; `ROAD-68-H` closed on `road68-H`. Round 7 (2026-10-29): the plants on the road after an undo or commit are the vegetation's own lag (its queue updates an edit's patches up to 42 frames after the click), not the undo hold; carried to T65 as its third layer, no code change; the user took the final tip's undo hold from the comparison clips and accepted the terrain stage's 3.01 ms catch-up frame as measured ([question 66](roads.md#road-68-open-questions) (a)), and `road` took H (2026-10-30). The same session attributed the economy contract's baseline-30k rise to
code before `ROAD-68` and found two supplier work-count limits that fail only on `ROAD-68`
cities (open question 20). Its attribution (2026-10-25) found no road defect: the counts follow
the city, whose 10k census needs fewer closure passes, and both scale within the limit per
pass; re-baselining the two limits waits for the user.
`ROAD-68-D` (terrain from the roadbed's outer chains) is done on branch `road68-D` (part 5,
2026-10-25): every
64 m CDT tile takes its road loops from its own halo rectangle of outer chains, holes come from
orientation, one source `RoadBoundary` replaces the legacy span and node sources, roads and sites
share one grading envelope, and edits stamp only the tiles their old and new pieces reach. Road
seams on tile sides are exact in both tiles. All 25 of its disabled tests are re-enabled and
the 24 that `ROAD-68-D` owns pass; the recorded Kuopio replay and the hillside fixture predate
`ROAD-68-C` and wait for `ROAD-68-E`'s fixture regeneration. Part 4 made the terrain watertight
(a conforming regular filler, no dropped small faces; `reference_junction_terrain_is_watertight`),
and part 5 the road surface and its seams (open questions 23 and 24, decided under delegation
and done): zebras paint whole asphalt triangles instead of cutting them, road chunks clip at
chunk lines and share the terrain patch's frame (`reference_junction_road_chunks_are_watertight`,
`reference_junction_road_and_terrain_share_seam_vertices`), and building pads are part of the
terrain patch mesh, which re-enables the Kuopio yard test. The reference screenshots' pinholes
went from 33 to 9 (part 4) to 0 (part 5). Still open under question 23: seam
T-vertices on straight edges at CDT tile lines and on one `y_60` edge, the truncated border
patch's frame and the shared filler/tile height function. Part 3 fixed the economy perf contract regression (a
quadratic orientation test in the grading pass, and building-site changes re-grading roads): every
timing is back within the control's range with identical work counts. The sloped T's terrain
stage meets its 2 ms target at 1.85–1.88 ms (open question 22, resolved). Span rows follow one
constructive station rule and terminal cap apexes are exact. Part 6 (branch `road68-D6`,
2026-10-25) makes commits work at city scale: a load or bulk road build no longer leaves its
compiles' terrain changes to the first commit (which then graded and built every road patch:
203 of 225 at 100k), and the CDT's tile clip no longer leaves zero-width rails to the tile
corners, whose made-up heights conflicted with building pads in 64 tiles of the 100k city
(`render_geometry_invalid`). The 100k commits are accepted and
the first commit no longer grows with the city (30k: 447 ms to 67 ms), but with a warm cache the
commit's terrain still takes 10-73 ms against the 3.0 ms budget, following the 512 m patches
the edit touches (open question 29). `ROAD-68-E` part 1 is in progress on
branch `road68-E1`, rebased on `ROAD-68-D`: the road tool previews synchronously on a versioned
context (published view plus a graph replica, no per-command city copies), returns the changed
road chunks exactly as the commit publishes them (crosswalk paint frames, lights and signs too
since `ROAD-68-F` part 1 builds them from the pieces), and a click installs the previewed pieces; Godot
shows each pose in the frame that sampled it and swaps to the committed chunks in one call. Undo
swaps the pieces back and restores the graph and lanes locally. The preview also builds the
terrain patches its pieces change with the commit's functions, the commit installs them when its
sources match, and undo puts the pre-edit terrain entries back. Round 4 made that terrain
follow the edit: the CDT input names chain runs by geometry, so a split trunk's far half keeps
its windows, and edits stamp only tiles near changed chain segments (or near changed pieces
where a site grades); the stage still misses its 3.0 ms budget on the sloped fixtures (open,
now with `ROAD-68-D` part 6). Round 5 rebased it onto `ROAD-68-G` parts 3-5 (on `road` as work
in progress since 2026-10-25), fixed entrances left on old lane ids after a lane-local undo, and
tests the terrain staging on the real terrain renderer; the stream at the CPU's idle clock
misses 16.7 ms on 38 of 864 frames because of the terrain stage (question 27, open).
`ROAD-68-E` part 2 (branch `road68-E2`, work in progress) implements the user's decisions on
questions 1, 2, 7, 11 (c) and 16: strokes read no piece their own edit rebuilds and meet a
crossed road at its graph profile, so the sloped T fixtures are independent of drawing order;
the commit refuses pieces that newly cover fields or painted cells (no more corridor checks for
them); node moves, crosswalk overrides and edge class changes are tried on a scratch roadbed and
refused when a node fails or reserved land would be covered; a stroke from a road end joins it
like any node instead of re-solving the road it extends (question 30 (a), decided under the
delegation for review), so sloped Y junctions are order independent too. Round 2: a mid-span
meeting lies exactly on the first-drawn road's graph profile (question 30; order independence
there is not attainable under the crossing rule); terrain edits re-solve every junction's
profiles instead of leaving sloped junctions failed, and roll back if one still fails; a road
bulldoze is tried and refused when a node fails; undo checks its restored closure in debug
builds; the Kuopio reference save is regenerated with the load rebuild (question 8), and its
load test and the hillside houses run again (their refined-terrain halves are ignored under
`TERRAIN-07`). Round 3 (branch `road68-E3`, work in progress): the recorded Kuopio strokes
commit again (`kuopio_05` attempt 14's `MouthOffPlane` was the edit plan's excerpt holding one
ring of incidence, so a far node lost its pin; the terrain spike's 8% street-grid T failed the
same way, and flat ones committed far profiles without their pins; the excerpt now holds the
solve's 2-ring, which changes gate G's determinism digest and takes the 100k commit's
click-to-acknowledgement median from 80 to 21 ms), the hillside fixture is regenerated with
the load rebuild (same parcel ids and frontage points), the mid-span order test is replaced by
question 30's rule, and the preview shows field and painted-cell overlaps before the click with
the commit's own test (question 25 (c): the field index and a persistent cell-reservation index
are shared with the view in `O(1)`), and a junction plane is sampled at the mouth station where
that lies beyond 12 m (question 31, decided under the delegation for review: three recorded
Kuopio profiles had left the ground by 5.08-5.76 m beside steep junctions; the sloped Y probe
fixtures change). Open: question 21 (b) (lots from the preview need the whole cell store
persistent) and the slower first commit of a painted city (`surface`, unattributed).
Round 4 (branch `road68-E4`, work in progress) rebases round 3 onto `ROAD-68-F` part 2: question
31's sample also changes six of part 2's family probe entries (attributed with a build that
reverts it), and gate G's determinism digest is unchanged. Its part 2 makes the zoning cell
store persistent and shares it with the road tool's view (question 21 (b)'s first step; the
user decided the preview never shows the lots it derives); outputs are bitwise unchanged, but
the commit's `buildings` stage is 0.3-2.0 ms slower (open, not ready to land). The first commit
beside a painted city spends its extra time in the legacy terrain validation (`TERRAIN-07`).
Round 5 (branch `road68-E5`, work in progress) attributes that regression to the persistent
maps themselves, not to copy-on-write (a view that shares nothing is as slow; `Arc` values,
read-first writes and a plain-map generation index do not remove it), and leaves the store
design the preview's lot plan needs to question 32 (user, 2026-10-25: profile first, then a
plain live store with a journal-synced persistent copy in the view); every lot derivation runs on one
planner and one installer, bitwise. Still not ready to land.
Round 6 (branch `road68-E6`, work in progress) builds question 32 (b): the live cell store keeps
plain maps and the road tool's view a persistent copy that each publication updates from the
store's write journal, with one planner generic over both; products and determinism are bitwise
unchanged, and the hover-validation flake is root-caused (the test read the hover path's pending
answer as invalid). The user paused question 21 (b)'s preview half (question 33: no per-pose lot
planning; after `TERRAIN-07`, if the commit still misses a frame, lot regeneration is deferred
one frame like D7).
Round 7 (branch `road68-E7`, rebased onto `ROAD-68-K14` and `TERRAIN-07-A` as `road68-E8`) lands gate E: the copy follows beside the commit's graph copy on the
shared pool, so the tool snapshot is at its control's; the two unchanged stages that measured
slower (render publication, preview payload) do identical work in profiles, code layout under
question 36; and round 5's lot planner, whose parcel overlay tested every parcel it had changed
on each query (quadratic in a plan's new lots: a populated city's preparation took 3 s longer at
30k residents), now finds them by chunk, bitwise. Open in gate E: question 21 (b)'s preview half
(paused, question 33) and the terrain stage (`TERRAIN-07`).
`ROAD-68-F` part 1 (branch `road68-F1`, 2026-10-25) builds zebra paint, street lights and road
signs from the pieces in the emission preview and commit share, keeps the crossing contract
bitwise and keeps road-end lights clear of the cap.
`ROAD-68-F` part 2 (branch `road68-F2`, 2026-10-25) covers the road families without terrain:
roads without sidewalks, mixed widths and profiles (a straight degree-two profile change is laid
out with D4 tapers instead of a pass-through, and the span width taper is removed), bridge decks
and tunnel road pieces at node pieces and span mouths, and one triangle walk for the zoning,
field and roadbed overlap queries; abutments, portals and sites moved to `TERRAIN-07`, and a
bridge or tunnel stroke from a road is rejected until it lands (open question 19).
Round 4 (branch `road68-F4`, 2026-10-25) closes gate F: the 0.06 ms slower preview payload
measured after part 2 was a codegen accident in the road chunk router (the same chunks bitwise;
its inlining and the triangle clip's polygon copies followed the crate's codegen-unit
partition), fixed by compiling the router beside its caller and clipping in place; payload,
commit breakdown and determinism are at or below the control. After the rebase onto
`096df250` the legacy terrain stage is 0.23 ms above `road`'s with the same terrain code, the
same kind of accident (open question 36).
`ROAD-68-K13` (branch `road68-K13`, 2026-10-25) traced the `TERRAIN-07-A` census's tallest walls
to the Kuopio reference save: its control polylines and node heights are the recorded session's
stroke solve's (one 16% clamp, before 2026-09-09), which leaps 27 m in 6 m from two dead ends onto
a four-way 29 m above the ground; today's solve builds the same strokes on the ground, and no rule
bounds grade (open questions 37, the maximum grade, and 38, re-recording the save).
`ROAD-68-K14` (branch `road68-K14`, 2026-10-25) implements the user's question 37: a 1:3 maximum
grade refused as `road_too_steep` in the preview and the commit alike, a warning from 16%, and
profiles built within 33% where their pins, node planes and the 5 m source-delta limit allow
(`network/grade.rs`, shared by the stroke solve, the junction profile solve, the validator, the
road tool's warning and the replays' quality check); question 38's re-record (all 36 recorded
strokes commit, 70 edges, none over 1:3) is interim until the user's new Kuopio save, which is
pinned by the same steps (`roads.md` question 38). `TERRAIN-07-A` re-ran its Kuopio census and
edits on the re-recorded save (round 4, gate A done). Open question 39 (four re-recorded edges leave the 5 m quality
limit by up to 0.36 m in junction blends) is decided by the coordinator under the user's
delegation, 2026-10-25 night, for review: build the blends inside the source band.
`ROAD-68-K15` (branch `road68-K15`, 2026-10-26) implements it: junction blends of standard roads
are built inside the stroke solve's 5 m source band (one definition in `network/grade.rs`), and
an arm the band and the plane leave no blend within 1:3 is refused as `road_too_steep`; the
whole-graph terrain sync builds roads by the same rule (refusing a terrain edit that steepens a
road past 1:3 is open, `roads.md` question 37). The Kuopio reference is re-recorded and re-pinned (5 edges move by at most
0.36 m) and both Kuopio quality tests pass again. Its timing of the grade rule and the remaining
`ROAD-68-K14` items (old saves load as saved; the first planning pass) are recorded under
`roads.md` question 37. `ROAD-68-K16` (branch `road68-K16`) lands it on `road` after gate E and
`TERRAIN-07-A`, makes the stroke solve allocation-free with bitwise-equal products, profiles the
commit window for the `surface` rise `ROAD-68-K15` had recorded as layout without a profile, and
makes saves byte-deterministic (lane connections were written in hash-map order; `simulation.md`
"Save bytes"); the Kuopio reference is re-pinned for that row order alone (`roads.md` questions
37 and 39). `ROAD-68-K17` (branch `road68-K17`) makes the junction profile solve, which runs
on every preview pose and commit, allocation-free the same way: its working collections stay
with each thread (163,224 allocations in the preview benchmark before, 11,619 after, none of
them working memory), with bitwise-equal products (`roads.md` question 37). `ROAD-68-K18`
(branch `road68-K18`) does the same for the roadbed's node plan, which placement validation's
`check_node` ran for every affected node on every preview pose (222,520 allocations in the
preview benchmark before, 69 after), with bitwise-equal products; it records which R-tree
queries depend on the tree's traversal order (two pickers' ties), the precondition for
re-indexing only changed edges (`roads.md` question 37).
`ROAD-68-K21` (branch `road68-K21`) builds the user's 2026-10-26 decisions on question 37: the
road tool's cursor snap and the bulldozer's road pick sort their R-tree candidates by edge id and
pick by exact distance, then edge id (the bulldozer's `f32::EPSILON` tie band is gone), and a save
holding a road steeper than 1:3 fails the load with `road_too_steep`, the rule an edit meets.
Loads repair nothing: question 8's junction repair and two dead missing-table branches are
removed, the doubtful legacy-load branches listed (`roads.md` question 37). Open question 48 (a
terrain edit can leave a road over 1:3, whose save then fails to load, until `TERRAIN-07-E`) is
recommended (a), taken pending the user's review; closed by `TERRAIN-07-E` round 1, which refuses
such a terrain edit.
`ROAD-68-K22` (branch `road68-K22`) lets the commit breakdown load its 10k, 30k and 100k cities
from snapshots keyed by the test binary, through the economy contract's snapshot code with its
exact-continuation check, prepared before a timing session; the products of a loaded and a built
city are bitwise equal. The check found that saves dropped the pending arrivals, so a game saved
with households on their way in planned the rest of its hour differently: save format 108 keeps
them and the Kuopio reference is re-recorded (`roads.md` "Saved cities for the commit breakdown",
`simulation.md` "Save bytes"). Open question 50: the breakdown runs the test build's whole-city
checks.
`ROAD-68-K24` (branch `road68-K24`) builds question 50's decision (a): the breakdown switches the
test-only checks off through the economy runners' `contract::timing_mode`. Its landing session:
products bitwise unchanged, the frames' agent time down by the lane check (up to 3.0 ms at 100k),
no commit stage moved consistently, and the 100k commit meets 16.7 ms only in sample 2 (open). The
populated city could not house the breakdown's default 1,300 and 13,000 tiers, at no commit
since the fixture's first: its grid's width is floored to whole blocks, which can lose more area
than its headroom; such a tier is now built one block wider and every other tier is unchanged
(open question 55, decided by the coordinator under the user's delegation, for review). The
wider 13,000 city exposed a fixture defect, fixed: carriers moved in at once kept a stale lane
bucket entry. The loaded city's cell store holds fewer entries than a built one only because a
build keeps empty generated chunks that a load rebuilds on demand; the products now count the
store without them (`roads.md` "Commit breakdown without test-only checks").
`ROAD-68-K19` (branch `road68-K19`)
makes the roadbed's coverage predicate exact, the defect `TERRAIN-07-D` round 2 found: exact
orientation tests on the `f64` positions, the closed triangle held (edges and vertices by every
triangle meeting there; confirmed by the user, 2026-10-26), no tolerance and no dependence on the
junction's orientation; pieces and saves are unchanged, and coverage and heights change only
within the old band past an edge. It also analyses `ROAD-68-K18`'s 10k commit-window profiles (not layout: the windows' work varies
between runs of one binary, open) and runs the full debug suite on the sorted R-tree query
(1,944 passed; `roads.md` question 37). `ROAD-68-K20` rebases it onto `road` `294fd0f1` and
finds the exact predicate slowing the graded terrain stage (100k sample 0 to 2.95-3.48 ms
against 3.0); the predicate prepared once per triangle, bit for bit the same, recovers most of
it, but one 100k run still takes 3.07 ms and 10k sample 1 stays 0.2-0.3 ms slower: open, not
landed (`roads.md` question 37). `ROAD-68-K23` rebases it onto `road` `6c51cf00` with
`ROAD-68-K20` part 2 (lane ends on their kerb's surface) and a fix to the validator's junction arm
angle, which differed by orientation (the 25° Y, at the limit, was refused in two of eight
orientations, on `road` too; [`ROAD-54`](roads.md#road-geometry-validation-road-54)). It traces
10k sample 1's rise to the coverage raster and makes the raster faster, bit for bit, but the stage
stays 0.12-0.22 ms over `road`. `ROAD-68-K25`'s landing session: the graded stage is under 3.0 ms
at every tier and sample from 0 to 100k (at most 2.72 ms) with every product as recorded, so the
branch is ready to land; the 3.0 ms stage, not `road`'s spread, is the target (decided by the
coordinator under the user's delegation, 2026-10-26, for review; `roads.md` question 37).
`TERRAIN-07-A`'s Kuopio census and edits ran on the save before `ROAD-68-K15`'s
re-record, which moved 18 profile points by at most 0.36 m; their re-run is open.

consumed the solve budget. `ROAD-67-C`–`E` are cancelled.

compiler, and superseded by `ROAD-68`. The parked `ROAD-36`, `ROAD-41` and `ROAD-43` are closed
as superseded. `ROAD-68-E` replaces the two preview modes with one road-and-terrain mode (`D3`).

| `ROAD-37` | `bug` | `done` | `P1` | The previous preview is swapped out under the result lock and freed on a bounded reclaim thread. It used to be freed under the write lock: 1.1–6.1 ms of serial worker time per result, and main-thread poll waits up to 9 ms (`*_cross`). The lock wait maximum is now 0.00 ms in every fixture, and products are identical. Freeing concurrently adds about 2–5 ms of cross-fixture compile time, which is accepted (see `ROAD-40`). |
| `ROAD-38` | `bug` | `done` | `P1` | The preview worker runs its parallel stages in its own Rayon pool, created once and sized like the global pool: one thread per logical CPU, or `RAYON_NUM_THREADS`. With four workers, frames over 33 ms in `flat_cross`/`sloped_cross` fall from 52–53/109–124 to 0/0, and moving age p95 falls 15–30 ms. |
| `ROAD-39` | `refactor` | `done` | `P1` | Explicit-step lower edges are indexed by raised owner in node export, replacing a scan of every step × lower edge per raised vertex. | `ROAD-40` | `refactor` | `done` | `P2` | Inline rounding (`x86-64-v2`, Godot's own minimum), fixed-seed foldhash node maps and fewer re-sorts; products are identical across stages and processes. Single-thread `JunctionN` compile −9% to −11%. Moving `worker_ms` −4% to −11% (`sloped_cross` 63.4 → 58.9 ms); each item was measured separately. Allocation moved to `ROAD-43`: glibc malloc is about 10% of compile CPU across about 45 sites. mimalloc was rejected after a matched A/B: moving `worker_ms` −6%, but peak RSS +31% at default workers and `idle_scaling/1000000` +23% (see `roads.md`). |
| `ROAD-41` | `refactor` | `superseded` | `P1` | Superseded by `ROAD-68` (2026-10-23): the legacy junction compiler is removed. Measured; nothing landed. A single `JunctionN` compile is a chain of 0.1–2 ms sub-stages that share mutable memo caches (`flat_t` 17.4 ms: rails 6.2, ownership 5.6, heights 2.0). Joining the same-band pair and raised-step sides kept products identical and gained 1–4% in the harness at four to eight P-core threads, but in Godot it was neutral: moving `worker_ms` −0.5% summed, with about 4% more CPU at default workers. So it was reverted; only the pool-based harness stays. Revisit only if a compile gains a coarse independent stage. |
| `ROAD-42` | `refactor` | `done` | `P2` | No-go. A diagnostic build offered the previous pose's topology cache to each junction compile, with identical products in a replay of the preview matrix drags. Cross junctions gained no hits, because every key moves with the stroke. Branch gained a few percent of lookups, for example raised-step source contacts 455 → 1,932 of 29,025. Moving compile time rose 0.3–2.3%. Nothing landed. Revisit only with contributor keys relative to fixed legs. |
| `ROAD-43` | `refactor` | `superseded` | `P2` | Superseded by `ROAD-68` (2026-10-23): the legacy junction compiler is removed. Measured; nothing landed. A `flat_t` compile makes 88,853 allocations (39.6 MB), and 93% of them are 1 KiB or smaller. They are stage outputs, keys, clones and nested `Vec`s over many sites, not recurring scratch buffers. malloc/free is about 12.6% of compile CPU. The largest reusable set, a reused i_overlay workspace, removed 10% of calls with identical products, and harness time was unchanged: total 0.0% and −0.6% in two A/B sets, inside the noise. Material gains need a compiler data-layout redesign or a different allocator (mimalloc, blocked by `CODE-15` and RSS). |

checkbox as the sole control for the curb-aware path. The Shift shortcut is removed; road
connections remain enabled. Grid directions capture only nearby cursor positions; other
angles stay free.

deployed. Locality measurements live in the owning zoning document.

lengths. Fresh verification passes 2,016 release Rust tests and both zoning bridge suites;
updated extension deployed. The owning zoning document records reproduction and measurements.

`TERRAIN-07-F` — `refactor`, `done` 2026-10-28; the real-display GPU idle-frame matrix passes
every pose (`city_low` graded -0.75 ms, six town rounds -0.16 ms).
Round 13 restores the shared-owner terrain-query index and fixes its stale removed-piece cause:
`precompute_road_mesh_data` published a new canonical piece map without draining the roadbed's
old/new changes into the derived index and edit journal. Both publication paths now use one local,
atomic boundary; the renderer-shaped regression fails before the call and passes after it, and
mutation products equal a fresh rebuild. Fresh 100k culled/full side means are 0.846/5.159 ms
control, 1.168/5.605 shared, 1.443/6.085 valid no index and 2.145/7.060 exact everywhere, with all
1,101 products equal. Shared's +0.322 ms gap passes the unchanged +0.42 ms bar and exact confirms
the LOD saves 0.977 ms. T45's minimum far clearance is 0.019999999553 m. Fresh 100k full-day
snapshot/frame means are 0.963/6.311 ms control, 1.227/6.609 shared and 1.569/7.181 valid no index;
shared's +0.264 ms snapshot gap passes the unchanged +0.40 ms bar. T44 keeps shared for 4.462 MB
at 100k; copied inputs remain rejected at 1.10 GB. `PERF-09` owns the control regression; no target
is relaxed. The user agreed T44, T46 (b) and T47 (a) on 2026-10-28, validated at `ROAD-68-H`;
T40 remains pending the user's review.
The round-13 matrix passed every pose except `city_low`; instrumented gamescope windows prove it
was not an upload or mask-render loop (uploads, same-level uploads, revision bumps and mask renders
were all zero). Grass instead rebound its five cell mirrors and their uniforms/textures every idle
frame. Round 14 makes that synchronization revision-driven and adds a 120-frame settled-view
regression. Two fixed gamescope rounds put graded `city_low` below exact, 3.20/3.05 ms against
3.46/3.87 ms; the real-display matrix remains the gate.
Evidence and acceptance:
[`terrain.md`](terrain.md#terrain-07-f-record).

Oracle-backed regressions cover a destination across the road, a car facing away, a bend, and a car leaving a building in the direction that avoids a U-turn. The dump replays without `no turn` failures. Routing benchmarks and `--perf-contract` are rerun. **Done 2026-10-15 (branch `u-turns`):** the destination exit turn is part of point and bucket queries and quote keys. The planner passes origin and exit edges everywhere. Same-node car trips are free only with a legal turn and otherwise take the shortest legal loop. Flow paths check both ends, and saved routes needing a forbidden turn are dropped on load. Randomized exit-oracle, across-the-road, bend, departure-direction, facing-away and load-repair tests pass; the across-the-road, bend, departure-direction and facing-away regressions fail on `9e70526f`. The dump replays 3 days with 0 trip failures. `./run.sh --test` passes. Measure states, arcs, build time and point/bucket query times against the recorded baseline on the 10k/30k fixtures and saved cities. The user decides acceptance before `NET-07` builds on it. **First pass 2026-10-14 (branch `u-turns`):** state-level order, minimum-cover separators and inertial flow cuts bring the dead-end-only split to 4.8× the baseline cost query at 30k (7.86 vs 1.63 µs; 9.5× with the old order), 5.2× at 10k and 3.6× on saved cities, with about 20× builds (52 ms at 30k). Tests and growth scenarios pass. Without the split the new order is neutral to 24% faster. `--perf-contract` (two alternating pairs): 30k hour and day +18%, 10k hour +10%, fiscal stress +1–2%, no new failures. **Accepted by the user 2026-10-14** and committed as `9e70526f`. |

Query costs equal a full rebuild's exactly, and paths, including tie-breaks, do not depend on edit history (the same graph gives the same routes, whatever the edit order). Determinism holds across worker counts and injected delays. Matched 10k/100k query timings show no regression. Interim idea: build the CCH speculatively from a stationary preview and adopt it on a matching commit. |

History: All four [exit criteria](economy.md#econ-13-exit-criteria) pass on one build. **`ECON-13-A`:** the 10k recipe stays within 40 ms per hour and 10 ms per day (latest routing build: 10.79 / 14.33 ms; daily bound fails). **`ECON-13-B`** (rewritten 2026-10-12; the same-build time ratio rose whenever linear work got faster): **B1** every bounded 11-day work count (census rows, copied rows, owner slots, proposed site rows, spawn scans, staffing trials, supplier searches/routes, job-search keys, CCH searches) grows at most 1.2× the household ratio from 10k to 100k; **B2** `/100k` stays within 10× A's fixed budget, 400 ms per hour and 100 ms per day. **`ECON-13-C`:** a profitability-failing dependency chain needs at most 3 funding censuses at lengths 4/16/64 (was one per link). **`ECON-13-D`:** after any frontage-delay revision, a warm queued check costs at most 1.2× as much at 131,072 background buildings as at 16,384. 1M stays deferred. **Latest routing status 2026-10-11 ([measurements](entrance_and_exit.md#cch-routing-performance-track-net-02net-05)):** `A` and `B` fail; `C` and `D` freshly pass on the frozen NET-04 revision. `A`: 10.79 ms hour / 14.33 ms day. `B`: 219.81 ms hour / 1,206.62 ms day at 100k, or 20.37× / 84.18× (one full pair, not acceptance repeats). NET-04 preserves NET-03 records exactly and lowers the 100k daily mean 6.2%; it does not establish an hourly improvement. `C`: two censuses at lengths 4/16/64, cold and warm. `D`: 1.07× warm checks. Earlier A/C/D acceptance and the 26× / 411× baseline are retained in economy.md as historical evidence. The remaining 10k day-15 spike is dirty lot regeneration; late 100k days remain open. The user authorized continued exact funding/lot performance fixes on 2026-10-11; economic decisions and job-lock timing stay fixed. Latest exact follow-up: household remaps, retained car membership, constant-time ring bounds and exact freight affordability bounds measure 10k 9.38/10.34 ms and one 100k run 143.02/252.15 ms (15.24×/24.38×). A/B remain open; records and all five reports are unchanged, with 2,322 library tests passing. Freight-door retention and shared household visit labels subsequently pass 2,324 library tests and all reports: 10k 9.46/10.02 ms, one 100k 136.34/256.70 ms (14.41×/25.62×), records unchanged. A/B remain open. Exact-price business masks pass 2,327 library tests and unchanged reports/records, but 10k 9.36/10.05 ms and 100k 138.15/255.01 ms establish no overall gain; the trial remains under review. Borrowed cell footprints and shopping-shortlist pruning pass 2,329 library tests and all reports, but 10k 9.48/10.10 ms and isolated cell timings establish no gain. Component-wide eligibility counts pass 2,330 library tests and all reports: 10k 9.28/10.03 ms, one 100k 135.22/215.71 ms (14.57×/21.51×), records unchanged. Clipped chunk scans, same-frame cell exclusion and cached shopping distances pass 2,334 library tests and all reports; two 10k runs meet A's budget at 9.23/9.67 ms with unchanged records. One 100k run measures 129.43/205.53 ms (14.01×/21.25×), preserving records; B still fails and final repeat acceptance remains pending. The workplace-cache hasher passes the same suite/reports and preserves records at 10k 9.30/9.66 ms and one 100k 129.00/203.78 ms (13.87×/21.09×); its isolated gain does not establish a significant whole-city improvement. Shared commute-cache stripes pass 2,336 library tests and all reports at 10k 9.26/9.62 ms and one 100k 122.96/201.52 ms (13.28×/20.95×), preserving records. B remains open. Proposed-site entrance reuse passes 2,337 library tests and all reports at 10k 9.24/9.91 ms and one 100k 121.72/200.62 ms (13.18×/20.25×), preserving records without a clear overall gain. Tied-radius maintenance and household walking-proof reuse are committed as `5a268e64`. **2026-10-12 ([work counts and live-book planning](economy.md#econ-13-work-counts-and-live-book-planning-2026-10-12)):** test-only work counters show the 100k gap is algorithmic: proposed site rows 67×, execution-epoch censuses 6.4×, market copies 12.8×, staffing trials 20× for a 10.2× city. Changed-owner site rows, a separate proposed-site index and live-book planning (pass journals with exact rollback) pass 2,344 library tests, all five reports and both populated records. 10k: 8.72/8.79 → 8.17/8.31 ms (four alternating runs each, p = 0.0286); 100k: 115.46/181.52 → 111.38/174.93 ms (two runs each). A passes. Under the rewritten B, B2's hour (111 ms) passes and its day (175 ms) fails. B1 fails on execution-epoch censuses (census rows 13.3×, owner slots 13.4×), staffing trials 20×, supplier searches/routes 16–20× and job-search keys 36×, against a 12.2× limit. Execution epochs now borrow their census books instead of copying them, so copied rows are 0. Paused 2026-10-12. |

The 100k 1× target is not reached: the removal window's first slot (3.3–4.7 ms), the census book's preparation halves and, on the cities this build prepared, heavy census slices remain. **`SIM-02-K` part 5 eleventh commit, done 2026-10-22:** the census's business claims run as a serial sweep that counts its scan events and carrier quotes, so no census slot holds more than about 2.2 ms at 100k (10.6 before); the removal slot keeps the zone index across its swap-removes and only returns the vacancy lists to building order and recomputes the admission order (slot 64 3.46 → 2.65 ms at 100k); both exact: the envelope is bit-identical to the tenth commit's, save format unchanged. In `PERF-07` the 100k 1× census-slice frames are gone (maximum 11.7 against 14.2 ms, a preempted frame); the 100k 1× target is still not reached: the removal slot with three removals, the census book's preparation (slot 944) and the first actions plan after loading remain at 8.0–9.7 ms work frames, besides preemption. **`SIM-02-K` part 5 twelfth commit, done 2026-10-22:** a removal finds the agents and households naming the removed or the moved building in one scan each and repairs only those (0.69 → 0.37 ms a removal at 100k), and the removal slot's terrain boxes, render state, refresh one a light slot later (slot 64 2.40 → 1.26 ms mean); the census book's preparation runs in four parts (pass and supplier rows at 936, now queue-free, their layout at 944, the rest at 953, the finance masks at 957; 944 2.65 → 1.69, 953 3.13 → 2.33 ms); the expansion sweep's priced routes weigh 230 work units (slices at most 2.20 → 1.69 ms); loading sweeps admission's border costs. All exact but 936's queue-free entry (envelope within tolerance, net income within 0.74%; bursts and determinism pass); save format unchanged. In `PERF-07` the 100k 1× frames over 8 ms fall from 31 to 9; the target is still not reached (in a schedstat day one work frame remains, the industrial planning setup at 8.0 ms; the actions plan is 2.9–3.0 ms). **`SIM-02-K` part 5 thirteenth commit, done 2026-10-22:** the planning setups scan beside their workforce count (industrial setup 2.16 → 1.49 ms mean, 2.90 → 1.95 ms maximum), a workforce reset passes over the buildings once, the site feasibility cache is split into 32 locks by parcel, the free-lot storage estimate is kept per parcel revision (a lending's site preparation 0.50 → 0.35 ms) and loading lends the live books last (the first plan after a load keeps its pass rows). All exact (envelope bit-identical); save format unchanged. A schedstat day has no 100k 1× work frame over 8 ms; in `PERF-07` the 100k 1× frames over 8 ms are 27 and 28 in two runs (control 20 and 35), and the work frames among the worst are the actions slot (9.3–10.0 ms). The target margin is not yet robust. **`SIM-02-K` part 5 fourteenth commit, done 2026-10-22:** no departure, admission or arrival clusters in the hour's minutes 3–4; the frontage delays sum by lane range on the pool in agent order (0.68 → 0.45 ms in every minute-first frame at 100k 1×) and each planning slot scans the free places beside its site preparation (plans 0.15–0.25 ms lower). Both exact (envelope bit-identical); save format unchanged. In a schedstat day every 100k 1× frame over 8 ms that carries slot work is preempted, and `PERF-07`'s 12 worst frames show the preemption pattern. **`SIM-02-K` part 7 (acceptance) 2026-10-22, stopped for review:** the legacy hour and day paths are gone (no `simulate_tick_internal` or `simulate_operational_hour_internal`; an hour's slot 0 holds only declared fixed slots, and its frames are no longer any run's worst); the envelope, determinism and burst limits pass, bit-identical to part 5's last commit; `PERF-07` meets 8 ms at 30k at both speeds and at 100k 1× on the frames no other task preempted, and fails at 100k 32× (mean frame 13.6 ms, worst 22–24 ms). Decisions needed: [open question 6](simulation.md#open-questions) (100k 32× needs a coarser tier for distant areas, recommended as its own item, or a cap on slots per frame, movement sub-stepping or GPU compute) and [open question 7](simulation.md#open-questions) (accept on the unpreempted maximum, recommended, or isolated CPUs, or fewer workers). **Done 2026-10-22 after the user's review:** `PERF-07` accepts on the frames no other task preempted, with the raw p99.9 and the preempted frames less their wait as guards ([open question 7](simulation.md#open-questions), option (a)); 30k at 1× and 32× and 100k at 1× pass it on both acceptance days (100k 1×: 7.35 and 7.73 ms unpreempted, p99.9 7.51 and 7.14 ms); 100k at 32× is deferred until players' cities approach 100k agents and tracked as `PERF-08` ([open question 6](simulation.md#open-questions)); `tools/sim02_gate.py perf07` checks the acceptance in every run. |

terrain/water uploads, terrain/water residency plus cache prewarm now use elapsed-time budgets instead of fixed patch counts, water steady-state residency follows terrain's resident-set revision, camera-near texture/LOD/mesh work first, water mesh ready polling adaptively fills a bounded camera-sorted apply queue with a conservative high-backlog headroom boost, terrain/water LOD refreshes are movement-gated with per-frame check/change caps plus stale pending sweep replacement and target-diff queue filtering, speculative prewarm is bounded to the near-resident halo, baked/CDT terrain patches skip no-op LOD mesh rebuilds, and water mesh refresh now polls ready work, applies ready uploads, then submits new work last under a soft scheduler budget. Large authored-map validation still needs fresh perf captures. | Baseline water rendering no longer depends on full resident-patch refreshes, shoreline/coast rendering remains stable, and any future flowing-water design starts as a new bounded system rather than reviving the removed prototype. |
| `WATER-02` | `bug` | `done` | `P1` | [`terrain.md`](terrain.md) | Kuopio water showed straight lake-bed slivers and hairline cracks along patch seams, and on steep banks the surface tilted up the slope. The imported DEM and the lake fill were both correct; three rendering faults caused it. The water shader read the terrain height texture (`4`-texel border) with the watermap layout (`1`-texel border), shifting terrain height up to about `28 m` near patch edges. Water border texels repeated the patch's own edge depth while terrain borders held the neighbour's heights, so surface smoothing gave each side of a seam a different height. Shoreline vertices took dry terrain height, and the mesh clipped shoreline cells where the zero-clamped depth reached its dry corner, so the surface climbed banks. | The shader samples terrain through its own layout; water borders carry neighbouring samples; every water vertex sits at the smoothed water level and shoreline cells are drawn whole, so terrain occlusion forms the shoreline and the shore fade uses surface minus terrain per pixel. The `issue-1..3` saves show no seam cracks and no tilted banks; wide lake views are unchanged apart from crisper shores; islands a few centimetres above the surface are now awash, as their heights say. Dry hollows below the water level no longer show a cross of water beside marsh samples (`cross` save). |
| `WATER-03` | `feature` | `done` | `P2` | [`terrain.md`](terrain.md) | Water reflected only a flat sky tint, lake-bed tree shadows showed through deep water because opacity followed the straight-down depth and was capped at `0.92`, and the lake was lit as a downward-facing surface (flipped back-face normal) with Fresnel pinned at its maximum. | The bed fades with the water the view ray crosses; a screen-space march reflects the shore and trees, accepting a bisected crossing only where the ray meets the surface (no bands on slopes, no gaps behind near trunks) and blurring by the ray's screen footprint (far islands no longer reflect white), the engine reflects the sky; physical Fresnel from world up; analytic wind ripples faded by pixel footprint. Matched M2 Pro idle benchmarks: `+0.5-0.9 ms` at water-filled 1080p views. |
| `WATER-04` | `feature` | `in_progress` | `P2` | [`terrain.md`](terrain.md#14-terrain-site-ground-water-and-lighting-materials-are-runtime-presentation) | The screen-space march cannot reflect what the screen does not show, and the shore behind a near tree went missing. A planar pass mirrors coarse terrain, tree impostors and, since 2026-10-07, buildings into a proxy world for up to two water levels. | Done: terrain, trees, buildings (one shared MultiMesh proxy per batch, within 1 km of the mirrored camera), fog as coverage, a horizon-anchored plane vote; since 2026-10-11 a shore cut that no longer stair-steps, steady tree scale, no high/low swapping between two lakes, and a camera kept 1.5 m above water. Open: bridges need Rust to export their deck, railings and piers apart from ground roads; hedges and roads on the ground are left out; docs and acceptance timings for the pass as a whole. |
| `ZONE-01`  | `refactor`  | `done` | `P1`     | [`zoning.md`](zoning.md)                         | Rust-owned `20 m x 20 m` default road-aligned parcels are the normal-runtime zoning authority. Godot submits input and uploads Rust-produced parcel overlay geometry. | Allocator and demand consume parcel legality, parcel occupancy is saved and restored through stable parcel IDs, and pre-zoned or free parcels can be created, extended, and rezoned without a zoning paint surface. Drag-run creation keeps legal candidates instead of cancelling the whole stroke at the first blocked parcel. |
| `ZONE-03` | `feature` | `done` | `P1` | [`zoning.md`](zoning.md) | Hover a road segment to preview parcels on both sides with the selected profile, frontage, depth and gap; press once to create currently valid lots. | Rust owns picking, deterministic layout and all legality checks. Manual drags and automatic fill share a layout anchored to existing parcels, respecting mixed lot widths and the selected gap. Slopes, bends and endpoint rounding retain the shared spacing corrections. Legal endpoint attachments round inward without cancelling both rows or weakening saved frontage bounds. Every density uses the same terrain check for the selected lot dimensions, independent of assets. Missing compatible assets permit terrain-valid zoning while growth waits for suitable content. Existing parcels remain unchanged, blocked lots are skipped, road previews cache by edge/options/dependencies, actual site failures use red previews without cursor warnings, and off-road parcel gestures remain available. |
| `ZONE-05` | `bug` | `done` | `P1` | [`zoning.md`](zoning.md#road-commit-cell-overlay-latency--2026-09-30) | Cells beside a newly built road appeared 9–15 frames (≈170–300 ms) after the road. | `RENDER-01` | `feature` | `in_progress` | `P2` | [`terrain.md`](terrain.md) | The world had one fixed sun angle and one fixed palette, so the simulation's operational clock had no visible consequence and every screenshot read as the same hour. A day/night cycle is now live: `day_cycle.gd` maps the day fraction to a solar position and an elevation-keyed palette, a single key light carries the sun by day and the moon at night (a phased moon since `RENDER-24`), and terrain/water/site ground follow through shader globals. New games start at `07:30` rather than midnight. A 2026-09-19 change separates the clock from the light. The cycle is cosmetic, and a player who does not want to play through night still wants the city to run through it, so `Tools > Time of Day` holds the rendered daylight at `Morning`, `Midday`, `Afternoon`, `Evening` or `Midnight` while the simulation clock keeps moving, and `Follow Clock` returns to the cycle. The hours live in `day_cycle.gd` beside the latitude and declination that give them their meaning: at `60 N` and a late-spring declination they put the sun at `12.9`, `45.0`, `27.8`, `2.3` and `-15.0` degrees, so evening is golden hour and midnight is moonlit. The menu drives `SceneLighting.pin_hour_of_day`, which already existed for capture tooling, so nothing new samples the palette. Still open: no seasonal declination sweep, no street or window lighting at night, the choice is not saved with the game, and the laptop cost is unmeasured. | Lighting is a pure function of the simulation clock, the whole day sweeps without a visible discontinuity, no key light shines up through the ground, night stays readable without street lighting, and the cycle costs no measurable GPU frame time against the fixed-sun baseline. |
| `RENDER-02` | `bug` | `open` | `P2` | [`terrain.md`](terrain.md) | Vegetation level-of-detail is decided once per terrain patch, because Godot tests one distance for a whole `MultiMeshInstance3D` and one instance carries a whole `510 m` patch. A plant therefore gets the level its patch centre asks for, with up to `361 m` of error, and half a square kilometre of vegetation switches as a unit. Three symptoms follow. Understory appears as a curtain when a patch crosses `BUSH_RANGE_M`, and near trees and every vegetation shadow appear together at `TREE_NEAR_M`, so one zoom step can cross both. Branched near crowns are drawn out to `800 m`, where their detail is smaller than a pixel and aliases. And `TREE_NEAR_M` cannot be shortened to fix that, because a band narrower than the patch diagonal (`721 m`) would let a patch draw distant crowns for plants `39 m` from the camera. A crossfade is not the answer: `VISIBILITY_RANGE_FADE_SELF` alpha-blends the whole instance and drops it from the depth pre-pass, which cost 17-28% of frame time and was removed. The aerial seam at `TREE_NEAR_M` exchanges four things at once: scissored foliage coverage for a solid shell, palette-gated backlight for a plain material, moving branches/cards for static crowns, and a branch skeleton for a lathe. Mid/far also collapse all variants to zero, losing pine silhouettes. The earlier description incorrectly treated this as only a mesh-authoring gap. A 2026-09-13 headless CPU reference raster at equal apparent height measures near→mid coverage steps of +52.18/+52.06 percentage points (conifer/broadleaf); opaque-card controls account for most of that, while backlight is small under the fixed test light and conifer width still changes by 8.82 pixels. A shared distant scissor/backlight material first reduced those coverage steps to +4.52/-1.05 points. The filtered-hash follow-up suppresses and darkens distant wood, uses derivative-selected blended octaves and measures +3.65/+1.73 near→mid and -3.17/-1.05 mid→far points at 16 px, without changing ranges, near meshes, surfaces or primitives; RGB-vector near→mid error decreases for both species, with individual colour-channel regressions. The raster then found the larger defect underneath: the near crown filled only `.1417`/`.1527` of its own silhouette against `.5533`/`.6560` for the same meshes with opaque cards, so the baked foliage mask, not the geometry, was removing three quarters of the canopy, and near fill fell with distance because a card's cluster vanished outright once a mip held one texel per cell. With the mask fitted to its cell, densified and made to fill rather than vanish, near fill becomes `.4162`/`.5003` and stops falling. Those coverage figures were then found to be measured through a blind spot: `coverage` is fill of a crown's own bounding box and the raster scales every level to one apparent height, so both of a crown's size terms divide out and two levels can agree on coverage while one hides half the ground the other does. A `footprint` metric in world metres now records beside it, and it showed the distant lathe was built at the branch base rather than at the foliage extent, at `4.70 m` against a `9.68 m` near conifer crown. Sizing the distant crowns from the measured near foliage envelope moves footprint against the near crown from `52.8`/`47.1` to `101.7`/`93.0` percent for conifer and from `47.2`/`57.6` to `103.9`/`88.4` for broadleaf, adding no triangle at any level. Paired `E13` GPU captures on a GTX 1060 3GB price the change at `-0.098` to `+0.224 ms` of scatter cost across five views, three of them negative, below the `0.25 ms` within-trial spread, with one extra draw call on two views and identical video memory; on screen the distant canopy darkens the ground it stands on by `42%` more. Colour, wind, variant and patch-granularity discontinuities remain. See [the measurement tables and limits](terrain.md#vegetation-lod-reference-measurements--render-02-2026-09-13). A 2026-09-16 change takes the colour half. The key light runs its cascades to `SHADOW_MAX_DISTANCE_M` (`420 m`), so every crown past that distance is lit on each surface the sun can reach and a stand loses the shadows its own members cast on each other; that loss begins at the cascade edge, not at the mesh change at `TREE_NEAR_M`. All three vegetation shaders now multiply albedo and backlight by one per-instance canopy shade term that fades in exactly where the cascades fade out, so nothing inside shadow range changes. The term is smooth value noise over `48 m` cells keyed on the instance origin, which the near and the distant mesh of one tree share, so a tree keeps its shade across the level switch. Its mean is `0.70`, the share a canopy keeps when about two fifths of its visible surface sits in shadow at ambient level. A paired synthetic overhead capture on a GTX 1060 3GB measures the nearest rows unchanged to four decimal places and the mid and far canopy `10-19%` darker; `METRUM_CANOPY_SHADE` scales the term, with `0` removing it. The patch-granularity defect above is untouched. A 2026-09-16 probe then found a second defect under the patch-granularity one, and it removes trees rather than shading them. Godot measures a visibility range from the instance bounds and not from the node origin: a real-renderer probe put two `MultiMeshInstance3D` at the camera's own position and culled the one whose single instance sat past the range. Each near level holds one variant and the distant level holds the whole species, so their bounds centres differ. On a random scatter of `396` stems in `12` variants that offset is `12 m` on average, `24 m` at the 90th percentile and `67 m` at worst. The `(0, TREE_NEAR_M)` and `(TREE_NEAR_M, TREE_FAR_M)` ranges are complementary by construction and were not complementary in practice: across the metres between the two centres the distant level had already stopped and the near level had not yet started, and the trees of that variant were drawn by neither. About half the variants sit on the losing side of that, which thins the canopy as the camera closes on `TREE_NEAR_M` and fills it back in a moment later. A paired probe on a GTX 1060 3GB reproduced it: both levels drew `0` pixels at a camera position between the two centres, and one level drew `72` pixels again after both were given the union of their bounds. Every level of a patch now takes that union as its `custom_aabb`, computed once per upload and never per frame. The headless dummy renderer reports empty multimesh bounds, so the regression holds the shared-bounds contract and not a measured box. The reference raster prices what the switch still exchanges: at `16-24 px` the distant conifer crown covers `107.1%` of the near crown footprint and the distant broadleaf `105.5%`, so the two representations differ by under `8%` once both are actually drawn. Patch granularity, the understory curtain at `BUSH_RANGE_M` and on-screen appearance are untouched. A 2026-09-16 capture over a brush-painted dense stand (`imgs/reference/game/16-09-lod-blocky*.png`) shows the patch boundary as a staircase of `510 m` squares between a dark closed canopy and a pale field, and that capture rules the crown representation out as the cause of the pale. Three fresh measurements: the distant crown colour is the area-weighted mean of the near mesh, and restricting that mean to foliage moves it by `4.1%` for both species, because wood is only `8.7%`/`3.1%` of near crown area; the mean lit RGB per covered pixel at `20 px` agrees between the near crown and the distant crown within `10%`; and the near-crown footprint the distant crown covers rises with camera elevation, from `107.1%`/`105.5%` at `45 degrees` to `116.4%`/`128.1%` at `65` and `124.6%`/`152.4%` at `80`. The distant crown is therefore brighter and larger than the near crown from a high camera, which is the wrong sign for a pale far field, so the missing dark mass is the understory that is built only inside `UNDERSTORY_PATCH_RANGE_M` and drawn only to `BUSH_RANGE_M`. The raster now takes an `--elevation` argument, because the two representations agree at one elevation and not at another. Two changes follow, both deterministic and neither costing a frame. Bush and rock carry a single level, so a variant that stops early drops nothing into a gap; each of the twelve variants now ends at its own share of the range, spread by a low-discrepancy sequence over the last quarter of it, so the carpet dissolves over `105 m` instead of ending on one line. The shared-bounds merge above is also narrowed from the patch to one species, because the complementary pair is a species' own near and distant levels and a bush must not measure its `420 m` range from a box that holds a `25 m` canopy. And each patch now swaps its canopy on its own distance, `TREE_NEAR_M` less up to `CANOPY_SWITCH_JITTER_M` (`160 m`) from a hash of the patch key, so the switch circle is no longer one circle. The offset is negative only, which leaves `_near_band_wanted` the conservative superset it already was and draws the near level over less ground, never more. The colour step itself is reduced, not removed. The remaining suspects are the understory mass and the ground shadow that ends with the cascades at `SHADOW_MAX_DISTANCE_M`, neither of which has a paired in-engine measurement yet. A 2026-09-19 capture (`imgs/reference/game/19-09-lod-issue.png`) names the second suspect and it is confirmed. `RENDER-05` publishes forest-floor land cover at unchanged albedo luminance, on the rule that only real tree shadows may darken ground. That rule holds inside the cascades and fails outside them: past `SHADOW_MAX_DISTANCE_M` no tree shadow exists, so the floor of a closed stand returns to open-meadow brightness while the crowns standing on it keep the canopy shade term added on 2026-09-16. The far field therefore reads as lit meadow carrying dark specks where the near side of the same stand reads as closed forest, which is the colour step the screenshots show. The terrain shader now takes the receiver half of the same term: one ramp over the same `327.6-420 m` fade, scaling albedo toward `CANOPY_FLOOR_SHADE_FLOOR` in proportion to the crown coverage the shader already fetches. That floor is derived and not chosen, as `(GROUND_SHADOW_AMBIENT + GROUND_SHADOW_SUN_STRENGTH * GROUND_SHADOW_MIN_VISIBILITY) / (GROUND_SHADOW_AMBIENT + GROUND_SHADOW_SUN_STRENGTH)`, or `0.51`, which is what ground the cascades did darken keeps where sun and sky are of comparable strength. Coverage is the union of accepted crown discs, so a lone tree on a meadow darkens almost nothing, a generated stand at `30.5` stems/ha reaches about `8%` coverage and a brush-painted stand at `625` stems/ha reaches about `83%`. The cost is one smoothstep, one mix and one multiply on a fetch the shader already made, and the ramp is radial about the camera, so it adds no patch edge. `METRUM_CANOPY_SHADE` now scales both halves of the term, which moved to `SceneLightingConfig.canopy_shade_strength`. A rendered `gl_compatibility` regression measures the contract on the GTX 1060: ground with no coverage is identical at `10 m` and at `600 m`; covered ground at `10 m` is identical with the term at `0` and at `1`; and covered ground at `600 m` measures `0.3203` against `0.6480`, a ratio of `0.494` against the `0.51` linear floor, the difference being tone mapping. Patch granularity is untouched. A 2026-09-19 capture after that change (`imgs/reference/game/19-09-lod-after-fix.png`) still shows the step, and a first in-engine measurement then found why a week of work did not move it. Every colour figure above comes from `tools/vegetation_lod_measure.py`, a CPU raster under a fixed test light with no engine ambient, no engine backlight and no tone map. It reported the two levels agreeing on lit RGB within `10%`. A probe that puts both levels on the shipped GPU with the shipped shaders over one population of `625` stems/ha measures the distant level at `1.28x` the near level's crown luminance for conifer and `1.36x` for broadleaf, and that ratio holds at `380 m`, `900 m` and `1800 m`. Distance is not the variable: the same near level changes by `8%` across that whole range, so neither the cascade edge nor the canopy shade term is the cause. The same probe measures crown ground coverage at `0.92-0.98` for all three levels at that density, so coverage is not the cause either. The cause is that the levels were fitted on albedo when the invariant is rendered radiance: the near crown is a cloud of cards whose normals point everywhere, which shadow each other and hide a dark interior, while the distant crown is one convex lathe whose every visible face turns outward and takes the key light almost fully, so one mean albedo on the lathe draws far brighter than the crowns it stands in for. `DISTANT_RADIANCE_MATCH` now scales distant albedo and backlight per species. The values are fitted and not derived, because the tone map makes rendered luminance sublinear in albedo: a sweep puts them at `0.558` and `0.505`, which bring the ratio to `1.037` and `1.006`. `vegetation_level_match_test` holds this on a real display and fails at `1.322`/`1.360` with the term removed. The CPU raster keeps its use for silhouette and footprint work and is no longer evidence about colour. A 2026-09-19 change takes the third of the four things the level switch exchanges, movement. Sway is authored in metres and drawn in pixels and nothing connected the two: the term is bounded by `0.50 m` and applies unchanged to `TREE_NEAR_M`, where one pixel covers `1.14 m` of world at `1080p` and the engine's default `75 degree` field of view, so the whole motion is under half a pixel. Motion that small cannot move a crown's outline; it can only flip alpha-scissor pixels along every cutout edge in the stand, which is what a stand seen from altitude reads as a boil rather than as wind. A GPU probe over `625` stems/ha prices it: with no gate, `42%` of the stand's crown pixels change by more than `0.02` luminance between a still and a displaced stand at `1500 m`, where the sway measures `0.18 px`, against `57%` at `150 m` where it measures `1.83 px`. Both shaders that displace now scale the offset by a screen-space gate. A first pass ramped `smoothstep` over `0.6` to `2.0` pixels of that `0.50 m`, full sway inside `176 m` and none past `586 m`, and measured `55%` at `150 m`, `13%` at `300 m` and `0%` from `500 m` out. That still read as shimmer in play across the middle distance, which says one pixel of offset is where the failure begins and not where it ends: between one and two pixels the sway moves a crown by a fraction of its own width, which a single tree reads as motion and a closed stand reads as the same shimmer with a longer period. The band is therefore authored past the resolution floor rather than at it, as `smoothstep` over `1.4` to `4.4` pixels raised to the power `1.30`, the exponent keeping the tail short instead of trailing a few percent of sway across the middle distance. At `1080p` and `75 degrees` that is full sway inside `80 m` and none past `251 m`. Measured after the retune over the same `625` stems/ha stand: `49%` of crown pixels move at `100 m`, `31%` at `150 m`, `8%` at `200 m`, `0.5%` at `250 m` and none at `300 m`, against `42%` at `1500 m` with no gate at all, so close-up liveliness is kept and the boil is gone. `PROJECTION_MATRIX` reads as a zero matrix in the vertex stage, which silently removed the wind from the whole game on the first attempt and which neither shader compilation nor any existing test caught, so the one projection term comes from `CAMERA_FOV_DEG` as a uniform while `VIEWPORT_SIZE`, which is populated, supplies the rest and a window resize needs no update. A shadow-pass check confirms a tree and its shadow still displace together. `vegetation_wind_gate_test` holds both ends of the band on a real display, at `55 m` and `300 m` through its own `768 px` and `70 degree` optics, and fails with the gate forced to `1.0` and with it forced to `0.0`. Wind exists only on the near level, so this also quiets the `TREE_NEAR_M` boundary, where movement previously stopped dead. Patch granularity is untouched. A 2026-09-22 third canopy level (index 1) keeps every card and cuts the interior wood, at 41 to 50 percent of level 0 triangles and `25.8 ms` of a close painted-stand frame. It is a per-patch mesh swap on the instance already uploaded, like the crown swap under `TREE_MID_M`, so it adds no instance and no draw call and needs no band wider than a patch; a populated near patch still carries 38 nodes. The distant lathes move to indices 2 and 3 with their calibration unchanged. `TREE_NEAR_DETAIL_M` is `45 m` and a nearer handover buys no further time. Patch granularity itself is unchanged, so this item remains open; see [the measurements](terrain.md#the-branched-tree-at-half-the-wood-is-worth-26-ms-2026-09-22). A 2026-09-22 rendered sweep found the colour step again from altitude: `DISTANT_RADIANCE_MATCH` had been fitted with the sun behind the camera, and with the sun in front of it the closed lathe showed its unlit half and rendered at `0.49`/`0.60` of the near level. The distant shader now tilts its normals toward up, drops specular and wraps diffuse per species, which holds the ratio within `0.103` of one over 36 camera and sun poses, and `vegetation_level_match_test` now checks all of them; see [the sweep](terrain.md#the-distant-crown-was-fitted-at-one-sun-2026-09-22). The same day the lathe was replaced as the visible distant level by hemi-octahedral impostors baked offline from the near tree, and `TREE_NEAR_FLOOR_M` moved from `800 m` to `250 m`; the lathe remains only as the shadow caster. The impostor matches the near level within `0.12` over the 36 poses, and E24 on the painted stand measures `40.0` → `23.6 ms` in the stand and `52.0` → `16.5 ms` from the air. Moving the floor exposed a latent rebuild loop in `canopy_near_m`, now fixed and asserted; see [the impostors](terrain.md#distant-trees-are-impostors-of-the-near-tree-2026-09-22). A 2026-09-23 change stops trees receiving cast shadows past `120 m`, where the solid proxy they stand in shadowed them patch by patch, gives the impostor the cards' sky reflection, and keeps five trunk sides on the reduced tree; see [the shadow fade](terrain.md#trees-stop-receiving-the-shadows-of-the-proxies-they-stand-in-2026-09-23). A 2026-09-24 change bakes one impostor per near variant and hands each tree over to its impostor by its own distance, dithered in screen space over `150-200 m`, so the canopy no longer switches a patch at a time; see [the per-tree handover](terrain.md#each-tree-hands-over-to-its-impostor-on-its-own-distance-2026-09-24). A 2026-09-25 change keys each tree's form on its world position, so a terrain patch no longer re-rolls every tree it holds when it changes between the fine and the coarse patch grid, and each tree now changes its shadow caster at `SHADOW_PROXY_M` by its own distance, so a patch changing caster changes no shadow; see [the grid fix](terrain.md#a-tree-keeps-its-form-across-the-patch-grids-2026-09-25). The same day the impostor is lit by each tree's own yaw, which it ignored, and its volume response is refitted over a second pose set at the handover distance from near the ground; see [the impostor lighting](terrain.md#each-impostor-is-lit-by-its-own-yaw-2026-09-25). Past `TREE_FAR_M` the ground now darkens by the share of each sight line a stand of the published coverage would stop, and each tree dissolves into it over the last `500 m`; see [the far range](terrain.md#the-ground-stands-in-for-the-trees-past-the-far-range-2026-09-25). | Vegetation fades in per plant rather than per patch, using alpha hashing so the material stays in the opaque pass with depth write intact; `TREE_NEAR_M` is short enough that branched crowns are not drawn past the distance they were authored for; and no single camera step makes a visible block of vegetation or shadow appear at once. An intermediate crown exists between the branched near mesh and the smooth mid cone, so no camera step exchanges branch geometry for a cone in one jump, and the level count is set by measured silhouette error against primitive count rather than by the three levels that happen to exist. |

| `RENDER-06` | `bug` | `open` | `P2` | [`terrain.md`](terrain.md) | Species do not read as the species they name.
