Revised 2026-09-19 against the shipped build: the pine gap this entry opened with is closed. `_is_pine` makes two of every three conifer variants pine, `_pine_trunk` carries the long bare trunk, `PINE_CROWN_BARK` the orange-red upper bark, and `_crown_reach` holds a separate curve per silhouette, so manty and kuusi are now distinct forms. Four items remain. First, spruce does not land: the drooping-bough sign is there (`0.12` for pine against `-0.14` for spruce) but the crown reads as a sparse dark spike rather than the dense narrow spire of a Finnish kuusi, which carries foliage nearly to the ground. This is crown density and lower-crown reach, not silhouette. Second, the player cannot choose a species: `main_ui.gd` offers `Conifer`, `Broadleaf`, `Bush` and `Rock`, and the pine/spruce and birch/aspen choice is made by the generator from position, so a player who wants a spruce stand cannot plant one. Carrying a variant would have to reach the addition delta, and only the near level pays for variants, because mid and far share variant zero's mesh for the whole species; a player-placed spruce therefore reverts to the species form past `TREE_NEAR_M` until that is addressed too. Third, birch bark is too weak and too even: `_birch_trunk` places `8` bands at a uniform `0.10 * height` spacing from `7%` to `77%` of height, each `35-75 mm` tall, and the single dark ring spans `y = -0.4` to `0.35 m`, most of it below the ground, so a mature trunk shows no dark base. Fourth, the third broadleaf variant in each group of three (`_is_birch` is `variant % 3 != 2`) is a generic broadleaf with no identity, and aspen is the Finnish occupant of that slot. Measuring the meshes also found a scale disagreement that is not fixed here: a near crown spans about `4.7-5.9 m` of half width, while `land_cover.rs` splats the forest floor from crown radii of `3.0 m` and `3.5 m`, and a mature Finnish crown is nearer the second figure than the first. The drawn crowns are therefore roughly `1.7x` the radius the simulation believes they have, which widens every tree against the `30 trees/ha` of `RENDER-04` and is a candidate cause of the sparse, oversized read from the air. That radius is separable from the species work and is the piece worth pulling forward. A separate 2026-09-19 crown-cohesion candidate smooths foliage normals, removes per-face brightness variation and corrects two-sided card lighting; rendered regressions pass, and the user accepted the visual improvement on 2026-09-19. Geometry and species selection are unchanged; see the dated candidate in `terrain.md`. | Spruce reads as a Finnish kuusi from the ground without being named, and the brush can place a named species rather than a species group. Birch shows a dark base on a mature trunk, and the third broadleaf variant is an aspen. Each silhouette is traceable to a reference photograph under `imgs/reference` rather than to memory. Startup mesh construction and the per-patch draw-call count do not increase, and the `land_cover.rs` crown radii still bound every crown the renderer draws.
| `VEG-01` | `feature` | `done` | `P1` | [`terrain.md`](terrain.md) | **Must decide and must have before any upstream PR.** Vegetation is not world state. `get_decorative_tree_patch` is a pure function of position, documented as cosmetic only, persisting nothing; no save schema mentions it, no tree has an identity, and the player cannot plant, clear or touch a single one. The terrain shader then paints a forest-floor substrate from `woodland_field`, a hand-maintained GLSL mirror of the same sine field, so the ground is coloured as forest whether or not one tree stands on it - disabling the vegetation renderer leaves the mask fully intact, and a per-fragment mirror of a procedural field can never do otherwise. Two questions are open and neither has an answer in the code: whether a world ships pre-populated or is planted by the player, and whether placement is per-tree, brush, or an auto-populate pass the player can re-run. Scale rules out storing a population outright: Finnish stand density over the default world is around `10^10` stems. Both questions are now decided: a world ships empty or generated, chosen when the game is started rather than baked into the `WorldDefinition`, so one authored world serves both; and placement is a brush that accepts a one-tree radius, with clearing the same brush. The generator takes `enabled`, `seed`, stand coverage and in-stand stems per hectare, and all four are saved state rather than video settings, because an addressable tree is gameplay state and must be identical on every machine. Coverage and in-stand density are separate axes with different costs: coverage moves the `woodland()` acceptance threshold and is free, while stems per hectare is set by `CANOPY_CELL_M` and is bounded by `RENDER-04` - 16 m at 0.78 acceptance is 30 trees/ha, and a real 500-1500 trees/ha needs a 2.6-4.5 m cell, 13-38x the instances. Coverage control also needs a field the current one cannot provide: `woodland()`'s wavelengths are fixed in world metres at 2027 m and 785 m, so across the 500 m sandbox world the field never completes a period and the whole map falls inside one stand. | Ground land-cover is separated from canopy occlusion and each comes from its real source: occlusion from the shadows of the trees that are actually there, land cover from the accepted population rather than from a field the shader re-derives. A tree is addressable state the player can add and remove, stored as a sparse authored delta over the deterministic generator - tombstones and additions, sized by player edits and not by stem count - persisted through the save schema, invalidated by an independent vegetation generation for the render patch that carries it, and covered by a test that adds and removes vegetation across a patch boundary and a save round trip — **done**, save format 61, with the tool, the ground-ring cursor and the independent patch revision all in place. The land-cover half now uses accepted generated and authored canopy crown coverage (`RENDER-05`), with both revision streams and headless edit/boundary regressions — **done**; on-screen appearance remains unverified. The generator is parameterised by a saved seed, coverage and density, reads them from the save rather than from constants, and produces a stand pattern that still varies across a 500 m world - **done**, save format version 60. Those parameters are chosen when a world is selected for a new game, from a dialog whose defaults and accepted density range are read from Rust rather than copied into GDScript, so the renderer's density ceiling stays a single number - **done**. The stand field remains a generator decision and is never mirrored in GLSL. |
| `VEG-02` | `bug` | `done` | `P3` | [`terrain.md`](terrain.md) | The bulldoze tool deletes one building or road per click and reports its target through `get_bulldoze_target_at`, which answers only `building` and `road`. Shipped: `plant_at` in `vegetation_api.rs` resolves the target after the building and road queries and never before them, immutably, over a fixed 4 m cursor disc that is at most four canopy and nine understory cells at the shipped spacings, at O(K + A) in those cells and with no per-candidate allocation; ties break by layer, then row-major cell, then authored order. Clearance is asymmetric on purpose, because a generated candidate was already cleared inside `evaluate_cell` and only authored plants go through `placement_clear`, so the target is what the renderer draws. Deletion calls the brush's `remove_at` at radius zero and stroke zero, so it writes the same tombstone, takes one undo entry per click and advances the same patch revision; no mutator was added and no save format changed. A plant sharing an exact position with another is refused rather than targeted, because that mutator would take both. The hover is a sixteen-point ring at the crown radius, drawn by the existing polygon path, so `bulldoze_tool.gd` gains only a `kind` branch. | `VEG-03` | `feature` | `open` | `P3` | [`terrain.md`](terrain.md) | The generator grows one forest everywhere. `evaluate_cell` chooses conifer or broadleaf from the same field over the whole world, with saved coverage and density as the only controls, so the wet-to-dry site gradient that decides which species grow in a Finnish forest has no representation. | `VEG-06` | `feature` | `done` | `P2` | [`terrain.md`](terrain.md) | Vegetation was the only authoring tool with no undo. `editing.rs` pushes an undo state for every road, building and terraform edit, but `vegetation_api.rs` never called `push_undo_state`, so a 256 m clear-cut or a misplaced stroke was permanent and `Ctrl+Z` silently reversed whatever unrelated edit happened to sit on the stack. | `WATER-05` | `bug` | `done` | `P2` | [`terrain.md`](terrain.md#terrain-07-d-record) | Found 2026-10-26 in the `TERRAIN-07-D` round 5 GPU matrix (saved sessions' camera paths, exact cut and graded terrain alike): Godot logs "Too many instances using shader instance variables" and follow-on `instance_buffer_pos` errors. Godot reserves 16 vec4 slots per instance whose material has instance uniforms, so the default `rendering/limits/global_shader_variables/buffer_size` of 65,536 slots holds 4,096 such instances; the water reflection's tree impostor proxies (`water_reflection.gd` `_free_tree_proxies` 3,223 of 3,782 errors, `_sync_proxy` 55) use the `vegetation_wind` material, which has an instance uniform, as do the trees they mirror. Graded terrain adds its cell `MultiMesh`es (4,181-4,535 errors against 3,782-3,808 with the exact cut). Instances whose allocation fails draw without their instance values. `TERRAIN-07-D` round 7 (2026-10-26 night): the round 7 matrix logged the errors at load in every session (the trees' `vegetation_wind` at `_ready`; Kuopio 18,400 exact and 23,584 graded, saved 21,371 / 26,581, town 7,055 / 7,287), and graded cells past the buffer drew with default uniforms, so their patches showed the sky (`kuopio_t2_wall`'s close views); `project.godot` now sets 1,048,576 slots (65,536 instances, twice the most a matrix session asked for), lever (a) sized from that count, and the reference render logs none. **Done 2026-10-27 (`TERRAIN-07-D` round 8):** the matrix re-run (tree `e5b673ad`) logged 0 errors in all 12 sessions, both terrain modes, the saved and populated camera paths included. The reflection's extra 5.8 M primitives of round 7 were neither the cells nor the trees: graded mode built road patches' `PlaneMesh`es on a 2 m grid (the exact cut's subdivision factor), and the reflection's terrain proxies mirrored them (7.34 M of the 9.04 M primitives on the saved `orbit_high`, the tree proxies 0.11 M); graded mode now builds them at the base lattice. Lever (a) leaves the reflection's tree proxies taking `vegetation_wind`'s instance uniform (16 slots each, inside the counted bound); proxies without instance uniforms (lever (b)) are not built. | No instance-buffer error on the saved Kuopio and populated camera paths in either terrain mode: the reflection's proxies take no instance uniform (or a buffer sized from a counted bound), recorded with the instance census. |

| `ROAD-54` | `bug` | `done` | `P3` | [`roads.md`](roads.md) | Found 2026-10-18 during the `SIM-02-I` part 4 gate: `road_junction_preview_test.gd` fails about 1 run in 15 (`_test_terrain_delta`, line 515: the second poll returns null). `get_preview_road_surface_result` and its parcel-clearance helper only try the core lock, so the hover path never waits on the simulation, and return null whenever the sim thread holds it, even for a finished and current result; the sim thread takes the lock every tick, paused or not, to build the render snapshot. The contract says null only while pending or stale. Reproduced on `SIM-02-I` part 3 and part 4 alike (2 failing runs of 30 each, isolated profiles), so it predates part 4. | A poll that finds the lock held returns a finished, current result, and `null` only while it is pending or stale; the hover path still never waits on the simulation. Done 2026-10-19: at the end of every simulation frame, under the lock and from the state the render snapshot was built from, the simulation thread evaluates the current result's live checks (corridor parcel and field clearance, plan state, why an invalid plan refuses) and publishes them (`PublishedRoadPreviewChecks`). A poll that takes the lock evaluates and publishes them itself; one that finds it held returns the published checks of its result (same request id and retained revision). A result compiled for an older road surface is stale and no longer evaluated. A frame without a current result does O(1) work and allocates nothing; with one, the checks cost 0.012 ms p50 per frame (0.123 ms with painted cells), flat from 0 to 100,000 background buildings. Freshly run, `road_junction_preview_test` alone in an isolated profile: on the unchanged code 6 of 20 runs failed (4 with the three refetch expectations, 2 with the line-515 SCRIPT ERROR), with the fix 0 of 20. With the simulation thread forced to hold the lock 4 ms longer each frame (a temporary diagnostic build), 10 of 10 runs failed before the fix and 10 of 10 passed after, each answering 10–21 held-lock polls from the published checks. New test `a_preview_poll_during_a_held_lock_returns_the_checks_of_the_last_frame`; 2,506 release library tests and a full isolated `run.sh --test` pass (run without a display, so the two Forward+ vegetation tests skip). |

| `CODE-17` | `bug` | `done` | `P2` | [`debug.md`](debug.md#shutdown) | Found 2026-10-19 in isolated `./run.sh --test` runs: headless Godot bridge tests crash with a segmentation fault at exit, after printing PASS (`growth_meter_test` on `SIM-02-I` part 4, `building_lod_test` on `SIM-02-J`), about 2 runs in 40. Both core dumps fault (`SEGV_MAPERR`) inside `_Unwind_Backtrace` called from `__backtrace`: most likely a crash or hang handler still running after the extension library unloaded, or a simulation or worker thread outliving it. run.sh stops at the first failing test, so the bridge tests after it do not run. | Shutdown joins every thread the extension started before the library unloads; repeated runs of the test exit cleanly. 2026-10-19: one reproduced crash (1 of 40 `building_lod_test` runs; 0 of 40 `growth_meter_test`), symbolized against its exact library, was the road-preview worker. `SimulationNode::init` started it detached; after the node was freed it returned and dropped the last `SimCore` reference while Godot unloaded the library. Debug builds really unmap it, because gdext's hot-reload workaround keeps thread-local destructors from pinning it. The `__backtrace` frames were Godot's crash handler faulting again in the unmapped code; the extension installs no signal handlers. Freeing a `SimulationNode` now sends the simulation thread `SimCommand::Stop`, disconnects the preview worker, joins both, and waits for the node's Rayon payload and water-mesh jobs (`AsyncJobs`). Its own `SimCore` reference is then the last, so the core is freed on Godot's main thread. The worker joins its Rayon pool (`JoinedPool`) and reclaim thread before returning, and the Scene deinit hook stops and joins the hang watchdog. Not changed: Rayon's global pool, which cannot be joined ([`debug.md`](debug.md#shutdown)). Freshly run with the fix in the same isolated-profile harness: 0 segfaults in 160 `building_lod_test` and 80 `growth_meter_test` runs. Five new tests (joined pool, reclaim join, job wait, node shutdown ordering, watchdog restart) and 2,526 release library tests pass, clippy adds no warnings, The first full isolated `run.sh --test` with the fix (under load average 16) then crashed once in `growth_meter_test` with a pool-thread signature: a global-pool worker was still in Rayon's idle spin (`Sleep::no_work_found`, `yield_now`, round 13 of 32) when the library unmapped, while the other 23 were parked. **Done 2026-10-20 (part 2):** Rayon's global pool cannot be stopped, so the extension no longer uses it. All its Rayon work runs in one shared pool (`thread_pools.rs`), sized as the global pool sizes itself and built on first use in the game and in test binaries; threads outside every pool enter it through `thread_pools::install` (the caller blocks, as with the global pool) and `spawn`, inside the functions that do the parallel work: the frame step, the render snapshot, cell chunks, the road commit, undo, bulldoze, loads and world resets, four edit hubs and about 40 leaves. The Core init stage disables the global pool, so a missed call panics at its call site; the Scene deinit hook waits for the pool's work and joins its workers after the nodes are freed ([`debug.md`](debug.md#shutdown)). Coverage: a child-process test with the global pool disabled drives 27 groups of core entry points, the simulation thread, the preview worker and a spawned job, and found 6 calls the audit missed; the Godot suites with the pool disabled found one more; after the fixes all 41 headless Godot tests pass with no global-pool use. Freshly run: 2,528 release library tests and the 4 growth integration tests pass; the `SIM-02` envelope and determinism gate passes, and its envelope, daily fingerprints and determinism record are identical to `ROAD-54`'s; clippy adds no warnings. In the isolated-profile harness: 0 segfaults in 80 `building_lod_test` and 40 `growth_meter_test` runs, and in 20 more of each beside 16 busy-loop processes; three full isolated `run.sh --test` runs without a display pass with no segfault and no global-pool use. A diagnostic `PERF-07` smoke at 10k 1× (two pinned runs each, not acceptance timing) measured a mean frame of 3.41–3.46 ms on part 1 and 2.38–2.47 ms with the shared pool (p99 7.1–7.3 → 6.1 ms, max 107–119 → 86–90 ms), probably because the step now enters the pool once instead of injecting each parallel loop from outside it. Acceptance timing on an idle machine is still to run. Gated on dev 2026-10-20 after `SIM-02-J` part 3: 2,531 library tests, envelope identical, an isolated `./run.sh --test` with no global-pool use and no segfault; `PERF-07` in one session against the previous build: means 26–31% lower at 10k, 18–23% at 30k, 5% at 100k 32× and level at 100k 1×, maxima lower or level (30k 1× 84 against 121 ms); `PERF-01` hour means 10–15% lower. |

### `EARTH-01`

- **Observed in debug logs**: elevated four-way road tests can report clean terminal topology, clean mouth seams, and `cdt_invalid_constraints=0`, while still showing high terrain seam steepness such as `cdt_road_seam_max_slope > 1.0`.
- **Scope**: this is not a curb / sidewalk ownership failure. The road-owned top surface and terminal sideskirts can be correct while the surrounding terrain tie-in is visually too abrupt.
- **Current state**: road-touched terrain CDT keeps grounded `Standard` seam faces on the ordinary terrain path by inserting explicit grade-limited guide samples around the final road-owned footprint, omitting source samples that would force an over-steep cut / fill tie-in, and exporting those widened source / seam sample decisions in terrain debug output. Ordinary standard/node seam sources do not emit retaining-wall topology; retaining-wall output remains source-gated for structural / future retaining cases. Synthetic DEM coverage still validates structural retaining-wall classification, while production authored plus compact imported Kuopio DEM coverage validates ordinary lower-shelf tie-ins, steep grounded terrain, raised standard spans, raised terminals / bends near ridge-valley terrain, raised multiway junctions, and edit-order-stable emitted terrain-CDT topology.
- **Closure**: the first imported DEM failure was an internal JunctionN road-owned chord reaching the terrain seam constraint set. The fix keeps final road-owned footprints authoritative, drops only constraints whose exact side probes both classify as road-owned, and preserves exposed seam constraints with source provenance. Future closure variants beyond the structural retaining-wall path should get a new `EARTH-*` item instead of reopening `ROAD-01`.

### `EARTH-02` road/site terrain follow-up

- The changeset audit corrects opposite-sign runtime mesh-part yaw and shares transforms between
  rendering, site support and frontage attachment. Duplicate selection/lifecycle/bounds paths and
  unused centre-normal queries are removed. Revision 13 and verification are recorded in
  [`earthworks.md`](earthworks.md#changeset-audit-2026-09-11); broader frame-publication work remains open.
- The neighboring commercial-yard slit is fixed by welding near-side grading samples before
  tile containment. Both tiles now retain the same point/height; the loaded scene's 27.7 cm
  T-junction disagreement is gone, without moving its flat pad or modifying assets. Revision 12
  and paired-side regression coverage are documented in
  [`archive/terrain_legacy_surface_2026-10-25.md`](archive/terrain_legacy_surface_2026-10-25.md#shared-side-yard-seam-verification-2026-09-11).
- The remaining small-shop frontage wedge is fixed by replacing estimated mesh-support extents
  with cached imported model bounds. A 10×10 m commercial regression covers both road sides and
  slope directions; the reported scene passes 99 seam probes and eight terrain patches without
  modifying assets or the save. Verification and startup/query cost are recorded in
  [`earthworks.md`](earthworks.md#imported-structural-bounds-verification-2026-09-11).
- The 2026-09-11 missing-patch defect is fixed in shared CDT noding: distinct intersections near
  pad corners survive cleanup, and invalid terrain still cannot publish. The synthetic regression
  covers constraint preservation, exterior coverage and deterministic ordering; current correctness
  and compiler-cost measurements are recorded in [`archive/terrain_legacy_surface_2026-10-25.md`](archive/terrain_legacy_surface_2026-10-25.md#roadsite-corner-noding-verification-2026-09-11).
- This closes that specific terrain-publication defect, not the broader `EARTH-02` work item.

### `EARTH-03`

- **Observed in debug logs**: arbitrary-angle junction mouths could report invalid terrain-CDT helper constraints and still show steep mouth facets after the ordinary grade-limited tie-in pass.
- **Scope**: this is not a render material, texture, contour, or retaining-wall issue. The roadbed footprint is still authoritative, but the near-road grading envelope must be generated from that final footprint and must not add constraints that can cross concave road-owned regions.
- **Closure**: grading-envelope generation now lives with `RoadSurfaceSystem` terrain-CDT export. It consumes the final unioned roadbed loops, emits deterministic grade-limited guide samples before ordinary source samples, constrains guide rails only for a single clean non-hole convex loop, leaves hole / concave / multi-loop footprint sets sample-only, excludes building-site loops from roadbed grading, and preserves exposed road seam constraints.

### `NET-01`

- **Root cause**: `collect_crossing_splits` in `rust/src/simulation/network/topology.rs` skips any segment pair where `v1.dot(v2).abs() > 0.98`. This guard was intended to avoid false intersections on near-parallel roads, but it also fires for two distinct edges that are drawn in the same direction — including when the player draws a road overlapping or branching off an existing road. No junction node is created, and the two edge objects remain topologically disconnected.
- **Downstream effect**: `freight_car_eta_between_buildings` calls `cch_graph.find_path` between the two disconnected segments and gets `None`. `try_local_supplier_for_resource` returns `false` for every local candidate. All freight falls back to OWA permanently — local supply chains never form.
- **Observed in gameplay**: grocery imported 474 packaged_food from OWA at 1.75× local price on first order despite operational local food producers; producers earned only OWA export revenue, insufficient to cover wages, trending toward bankruptcy by day 26.
- **Fix**: removed the `v1.dot(v2).abs() > 0.98` guard entirely. Truly parallel edges return `None` from `find_intersection_2d` (denom ≈ 0) so the guard was redundant for the intended case and harmful for shallow-angle distinct-edge crossings. See `topology.rs:459`.

## Notes

- The old numbered backlog and bug table are archived in [`archive/project_legacy_2026-04-09.md`](archive/project_legacy_2026-04-09.md).
- New work should never introduce fresh `item N` references. Use stable IDs instead.
- Keep roadmap rows short: describe the current problem and a visible exit condition, then put subsystem detail in the owner doc.
- The Criterion microbenchmark suite was expanded to cover `ACCESS_EGRESS` and `ACCESS_INGRESS`. Benchmark deltas against older saved results may therefore reflect a changed suite shape, not only a runtime regression.
